Importance of Focal Distance
Each marking head type has several possible lenses for use in various applications. When selecting a lens, consider the needs of
the application including the material that will be marked, the size of the marking, and any irregularity in the surface of the
product to be marked.
Figure 4- 12 on the next page shows an example of two identical marking heads with different lenses: a 100 mm focal distance
lens and a 254 mm focal distance lens. Focal distance refers to the optimal distance that the product must be from the lens for
the perfect focal point.
135
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 12: Focal distance examples, illustrated.
When the laser beam leaves the output lens the beam itself is wide and the energy dispersed. As the beam nears the focal point,
the beam narrows and the energy becomes more concentrated, allowing for marking of the product. Each lens has a tolerance
wherein the product may be closer or further than the perfect focal distance and might still be marked. This area of tolerance is
called the Depth of Field . In general, shorter focal distance lenses tend to have less depth of field so slight changes in the dis -
tance from the lens to the product may cause the product not to be marked.
Shorter focal distance lenses tend to have a smaller, denser spot than do longer focal distance lenses. This means that, at the per -
fect focal distance, the spot size of a long focal distance lens will be wider and have the energy dispersed across that wider area,
while the shorter focal distance lens has a smaller spot size with the energy concentrated into a smaller area. Though a 30 Watt
laser will always have 30 Watt output, the focal distance of the lens determines the size and density (concentration of energy) of
the beam at the focal point.
The total area available for marking at one time is called the Mark Field . Longer focal distance lenses provide a larger marking
field allowing for either larger messages or, in the case of products being marked while moving, they allow a longer period in
which to mark the product.
136
Rev AA

Marking Unit
Following are descriptions of conditions using a shorter or longer focal distance lens create.
Shorter focal length lens:
l Product must be closer to head
l Less depth of focus (tolerance)
l Smaller, hotter spot
l Smaller marking field size
Longer focal length lens:
l Product must be further from head
l Larger depth of focus (tolerance)
l Wider, less dense spot (good for delicate substrates)
l Larger marking field
Working Distance
The Working Distance of a marking head is the optimal distance from the front of the marking head (red emissions LED cover)
to the product. This is typically where the best print quality is achieved and prevents scratches to the lens that would occur if focal
distance were used as a measurement.
The following figure shows where the working distance is measured on the 7230/7330 marking heads.
F IGURE 4- 13: Measuring Focal Distance.
Table 4- 4 and Table 4- 5 on the next page show the working distance for each available lens.

Galvos
Marking of characters is accomplished due to the movement of two mirrors, one for the X axis and one for Y axis formation of
characters.
The use of an X and Y axis mirror means that it is not necessary for the product being marked to be moving as is necessary in
many other methods of marking. One mirror creates the horizontal portion of characters while the other is used for the vertical
portion of the characters.
The galvos for the laser system are installed into a galvo block by manufacturing and then the assembly is paired with a CP-
Light3 board. The set are tuned together in a fixture to achieve best print quality and the tuning is saved to an EEPROM on the
CP- Light3 board. The galvo block and paired CP- Light3 board are ordered as single part number and should never be separated.
Separating the pair may cause or errors and/or poor print quality.
138
Rev AA

Marking Unit
F IGURE 4- 14: Galvo location on the SHF100 marking head.
139

Shutter
The shutter blocks the beam from exiting the marking head when the laser is not in marking mode. The 7230/7330 employs a
mechanical rotary shutter with a magnetic switch to identify the open/closed position. The shutter is located in the marking head
ahead of the galvos.
The shutter and the shutter board are part of a single assembly.
140
Rev AA

Marking Unit
F IGURE 4- 16: Shutter assembly.
Control of the shutter originates with:
l Shutter cable carries signals from HIC_ CPA through marking head cable to shutter board
l 24VDC from Chameleon X28 connector CP- Light3 and then carried by galvo cables within larger marking head cable
The following connections are made from the shutter board:
l Shutter control
l Emissions LED
l Pilot laser (if installed)
141
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 4- 17: Shutter assembly with shutter board and connectors.
Pilot Laser
The pilot laser is an optional secondary laser that produces a visible red beam, but does not mark substrate. With the clearly vis -
ible red light beam of the pilot laser, system set up can be greatly simplified. Controlled by software, the pilot laser follows cutting
edges and reference points accurate to the millimeter. This provides the user the ability to check accuracy before any real mark
and correct the settings if necessary.
The Pilot laser is a Class II laser. Benefits of the pilot laser include:
l Precise check of the data conversion on the laser system before the marking.
l Facilitation of settings as for example the reference point setting.
l The pilot laser is mounted to the marking head and remains mounted even during normal laser operation.
l No complicated and repeated retrofitting necessary.
l User- friendly and maintenance- free.
l Powered on/off via user interface.
142
Rev AA

Marking Unit

Marking Head Cables
A long cable enclosed in the plastic sheathing of the umbilical runs from the supply unit on one end to the marking head on the
other. This cable connects:
l CP- Light3 to/from Galvos
l Temperature sensor (thermistor) on the galvo block to the CP- Light3 Board
l HIC_ CPA to the Shutter board
l HIC_ CPA to the Emissions LED board
F IGURE 4- 20: Marking head Cable connectors.

replacing the Marking Head Cable.

SHF60B AL- SP79191
SHF100B AL- SP79192
Emissions LED Board
The marking head contains a board with four integrated LEDs that illuminate when the laser source has power supplied to it.
These red LEDs are visible through a red plastic window on the front of the marking head and let the user know that the system is
capable of producing a beam. The Emissions LED board is part of the larger safety circuit. If either the Emissions LED board in
the marking head or the Emissions LED in the membrane keypad fails, then the laser source cannot be powered and an error will
occur. Sensing of the LEDs in the Emissions LED board is accomplished via monitoring resistance across the LEDs. If there is no
resistance across the LEDs the safety circuit is considered incomplete.

Customer Interface
(Internal Connectors)
Inputs and outputs for the 7230/7330 laser system are connected via the Customer Interface (CI) located on the Chameleon
board. Depending on the rear panel installed on the laser these connections to CI may be done via Quick Connectors that are pre-
wired from the rear panel to the CI or if pass- through cable gland rear panel is installed then connections are made directly to the
CI. In both cases the CI is the same and it is only the method used to connect that varies.
The CI is comprised of seven connectors.
Table 5- 1: Customer Interface Connecter Descriptions
Connector Description
X21 Suction Control
X22 Variable I/O
X25 Line Interface
X27 Variable I/O
X29 Variable I/O
X30 Variable I/O
X33 Internal Connection
147
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
CI Connectors Location
The Customer Interface connectors are located along the edge of the Chameleon board near the serial and Ethernet ports.
148
Rev AA

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Default Bridges
There are several inputs on the CI that are active lows and which when the input is 0V or not connected an error condition will
occur. Error conditions are avoided by placement of default bridges (jumpers) to hold active low outputs in a high state by con -
necting to 12V/24V outputs. These default bridges may be removed if external devices will be connected to the active low inputs
or may be left in place after installation if the active low inputs will not be utilized.
Table 5- 2: Default CI Bridges as Placed by Manufacturing
Pins Description
X25.9- 12 Trigger Enable tied to 24V
X27.7- X33.3 Enables CPD and FASS
X21.1- 2 Exhaust Error to Suction On (24V output)
X21.3- 5 Filter Full tied to 24V

X29.1- 7 Error Status Customer tied to 24V
X22.3- 7 Internal use
X22.7- 9 Stop marking tied to 24V
X22.9- 11 Internal use
X33.1- 8 External key switch (Input to Output)
X33.2- 6 Internal use
Voltage Supply for CI
The Customer Interface section must be supplied with power and ground either through internal supply from the Chameleon
board or from supply by the customer using an external source. Setup for supplying the CI with power and ground is done by
moving jumpers on X19 and X20 on the Chameleon board and by connection of external power and ground (external only).
More information on configuring and connecting the supply for CI can be found in "X19 & X20 - Supply Voltage" on page 87 .

X21 on the Customer Interface of the Chameleon board is dedicated to fume extractor signals. Both remote start function for a
fume extractor and monitoring of faults from a connected fume extractor can be accomplished through connection to X21.

Default Bridges
Several default bridges are put in place during manufacturing to hold active low inputs in the high state to avoid errors. These
are:
l Pin 3 - 7: None to Stop Marking (since Stop Marking will have 12V/24V applied None will share the 12V/24V)
l Pin 7 - 9: Stop Marking to Input None (will have 12V/24V applied by another bridge)
l Pin 9 - 11: Input None to 24V
If Stop Marking input will be used, remove the bridge to pin 7 and connect 12V24V from customer equipment.

Connecting an Encoder & Product Detector
The 7230/7330 supports only quadrature encoders. When configuring the Encoder in SmartGraph software the pulses per rota -
tion must be doubled in Product Registration/Line Setup. Do not double the pulses for the encoder in TCS or Clarity Laser Con -
troller.
Product detect signals must be PNP (sourcing). Pin 4 provides supply voltage for product detect and ground is provided on pin 8.
Encoder & Trigger Detection
F IGURE 5- 3: Encoder and product detect connection.
Figure 5- 3 above shows the typical connection for an encoder and product detector for the 7230/7330 . It is not necessary to use
the Encoder Index track unless needed in the specific application. The Encoder Index track may be used as a Trigger in some
applications, but in that case the Encoder Index track wire should be connected to pin 7 (Trigger) rather than pin 5 (Encoder
Index). It is also necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 so that the system senses a load between these pins.
158
Rev AA

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
The laser system inputs are able to identify automatically if an encoder or product detect are connected.
l If a product registration/line setup is selected that uses product movement with an encoder, the software checks to see if
there is a load between X25.2 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.6). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No Encoder
Attached). The same mechanism is implemented for the trigger.
l If a product registration/line setup is selected which uses a product detector (Rising/Falling Edge or External), the software
checks if there is a load between X25.4 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.8). If no load is detected, an error will be generated (No
Product Detector Attached).
The minimum load to be identified by the system for encoder or product detect is 20 mA. If no load is connected then either a
"No Encoder Connected" or "No Product Detector Connected" message is displayed.
No Trigger or Encoder Attached Errors
If using a dry contact as a Trigger, such as a fire signal from a PLC, or sharing encoder between two lasers,
then it is necessary to use a resistor across pins 4- 8 (trigger) or 2- 6 (encoder) on X25 so that resistance/load is
sensed by the laser system. This is only necessary in CMark versions older than CMark version 3.4. In lasers
with CMark version 3.4 or newer it is possible to disable these errors in the laser's Web Interface and no resistor
will be needed. See "Disable No Trigger/Encoder Attached Errors" on page 327 .
F IGURE 5- 4: A resistor employed to allow use of a dry contact as a trigger.
Beginning with CMark version 3.4, you may disable the errors associated with no load on Trigger or Encoder supply pins by log -
ging into the Web Interface and checking the box next to Disable Trigger/Encoder Detached Errors and then rebooting laser.
159
Rev AA

A typical use case for signals from X27 would be to monitor Good shot and Bad shot. Following are descriptions of inputs and
outputs on X27.
l Shutdown - 12V/24V pulse to this input will initiate shutdown of laser system. Same function as pressing Standby button
on outside of supply unit.
l PC Connected - Output is set as soon as a PC with client application is connected. Signal is High Active.
l Good - Indicates that the last marking has been completed without a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next
trigger signal. Signal is Low Active.
l Bad - Indicates that the last marking was not completed due to a warning or an error. The output is reset with the next trig -
ger signal. Signal is Low Active.
Pin 7 is bridged to X33.3 in order to enable the CPD and FASS safety systems. Do not remove this bridge.

Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)
Pin Direction Description
X29.9 24V_ CI

Input Descriptions
l Error Status Customer - Allows for input of error from peripheral device. This input was known as External Error on ACC3-
based systems. Input is an Active Low, so if it is not used it must be tied to 12V/24V. Bridged by default from man -
ufacturing to pin 7 to tie signal high.
l Error Confirm - 12V/24V input confirms, but does not delete or save errors that have occurred on the laser system.
Remotely completes same action as pressing Confirm button in SmartGraph or other user interface.
Output Description
l Error - The system is stopped immediately if an error occurs during the marking process. The output is reset with the next
Start, but may be set again if the error was not corrected. Active low signal.
l System Ready - Output when system has booted with no errors and is ready to receive inputs. Any error will result in System
Ready signal to be set to low. Signal is an active high.
l Ack_ Job_ Selection - A high level indicates that the job selection process is finished. Low level indicates that the job selec -
tion process was not completed. Typically used with External Template Selection. Signal is a high active.
X30 - External Template Selection
Signals for External Template Selection are received via X30. External Template Selection allows a remote device such as a PLC to
remotely change the current template. The external device sends a 24V binary pulse signal representing a number. That number
matches a template in an already compiled template list in SmartGraph. After receiving the 24V signal on the Strobe input, the
laser system changes to the template matching the number received.

X33 - Internal Connections
Most connections on X33 on the Customer Interface (CI) should be left with default bridges in place. The bridges in place on the
X33 connector allow for functionality of the safety system including CPD and FASS boards.
Location
F IGURE 5- 6: X33 location.
Description
Table 5- 15: X33 Pin Descriptions
Pin Direction Description
1 IN External Key Switch In
8 OUT External Key Switch Out
The only function that can be used on X33 is the Remote Key switch. By connecting remote switch to pins 1 and 8, the user could
activate laser power remotely. If this functionality is desired then either 1) the key switch inside supply cabinet must be bridged or
2) the physical key left in the ON (armed) position all the time.
Default Bridges
Do not remove default bridges (jumpers) with the exception of External Key Switch. They are responsible for functionality of CPD
and FASS (where installed).

No Trigger Detector Connected Error
If a product registration/line setup which uses a product detector (Rising/Falling Edge or External) the software
checks if there is a load between X25.4 and GND_ CI (e.g. X25.8). If no load is detected an error will be gen -
erated (E10814: No Trigger Detector Attached).
If using a dry contact as a Trigger, such as a fire signal from a PLC, then it is necessary to use a resistor across
pins 4- 8 (trigger) so that resistance/load is sensed by the laser system. This is only necessary in CMark versions
older than CMark version 3.4.
In lasers with CMark version 3.4 or newer it is possible to disable these errors in the laser's Web Interface and
no resistor will be needed.
See "Disable No Trigger/Encoder Attached Errors" on page 327 for details.

quick connector plate or the 4 way DIN connector and cable may be used in conjunction with other quadrature encoders.

Encoder Detached Error
If not using 24V to supply encoder, such as in situations where one encoder is shared by multiple lasers, it is
necessary to either use a 4.7 KOhm resistor from X25.2- 6 in order to create a load on X25.2 (24V supply for
encoder) or error No Encoder Attached (E10813) will occur in lasers with CMark prior to version 3.4. In lasers
with CMark 3.4 or newer, it is not necessary to use a resistor, but you must log in to Laser Web Interface and
toggle the Disable Trigger/Encoder Detached Errors option.

Wiring Laser Alert Light (Beacon)
There are two styles of laser beacons; an older style with 5 wires including power and ground and new style with 4 wires. The older
style beacon should be wired as follows:
184
Rev AA

Customer Interface (External)
Table 6- 5: Alert Light (PN 376178) Version with 5 Wires
Beacon Wire Color Connect to Chameleon Signal
Red X29.2 Error
Amber X22.8 Shutter Closed
Green X22.6 Ready to Mark
Black X22.10 Ground
White X22.11 24V
Table 6- 6: Alert Light (PN 376178) Version with 4 Wires
Beacon Wire Color Connect to Chameleon Signal
Red X29.2 Error
Amber X22.6 Ready to Mark
Green X22.8 Shutter Closed
Common X22.11 24V
The new version of the laser alert light with 4 wires can only use input signals of the same polarity whereas the 5 wire version had
been modified to work with both high and low active signals. The 4 wire version uses the off (not) state to drive the amber and
green lights on the alert light.
Using the wiring shown in Table 6- 6 above , the green light will illuminate when the Shutter Closed output is not active and there -
fore the shutter is open which occurs while in the marking mode. The amber light is illuminated when the laser is not Ready to
Mark. This state occurs when the Stop signal has been received and no errors are present. Both the red and amber lights will be lit
when an error occurs due to the low states of both the Error and Ready to Mark signals.

l 45 mA max for Amber
l 50 mA max for Red

Fume Extractor Quick Connector
An external jumper plug accompanies all 7230/7330 laser systems that are equipped with the quick connector panel. This jumper
plug can also be used to connect a fume extractor to the laser system. Connection allows the fume extractor to be remotely star -
ted when the laser is in the Ready to Mark or other desired state so that it is not necessary to start and stop the fume extractor
manually.
You can also order the fume extractor jumper plug (PN: AL- 76119) as a separate spare part for use during service/maintenance.
Connection requires use of 0.9 mm Allen wrench.

Installation
The laser system accessories can be connected to the system both externally and internally. Externally, accessories connect via a
quick connector plate. Internally, accessories are connected via a cable passage plate or via the standard hybrid connector plate .
When using the hybrid connector plate, some accessories are connected at the Chameleon Customer Interface while others are
connected on external Quick Connectors.
Detailed descriptions of available inputs and outputs are detailed in the Customer Interface (Internal Connectors) section. The
Customer Interface (External) section contains information on how to connect, but not detailed descriptions on all inputs and
outputs available in the system.
Installation Pre- Checks
Shock Hazard Warning
Use test equipment safely and properly.
1. Check the incoming voltage from the mains power receptacle with a multimeter.
2. Ensure placement of laser will allow for proper airflow and cable allowances as shown in the Operating Instructions.
3. Check the laser shipping carton for damage. Check the shock/tilt watches on packaging.
4. Unpack the laser and check the condition of laser system.
5. Verify that the laser system in the box is correctly configured as ordered (model, IP, wavelength, marking head, lens).
6. Check that all ordered accessories are included.
7. Connect the laser system to a temporary power supply.
8. Position a target at the correct focal distance from marking head.
9. Create a Test Pattern template in SmartGraph.
10. Take sample mark on the target using the Test Pattern template. Does the laser mark the product as expected?
If the laser does not create a mark with no peripheral devices (encoder, product detector, etc.) then do not proceed with
installation until the issue is resolved.
11. Mount the laser. The mounting hole pattern can be found in the Operating Instructions for each laser model.
12. Set the customer interface voltage source on X19 and X20. The default configuration is 24V Internal source. See "X19 &
X20 - Supply Voltage" on page 87 .
13. Connect the accessories. See "Customer Interface (Internal Connectors)" on page 147 for more on connecting when using
cable gland plate and "Customer Interface (External)" on page 179 when using quick connector plate.
14. Connect the safety system.
189
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
For 7230/7330 safety connection see "SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring" on the facing page and "Safety System Installation - CPD
and FASS" on page 195 .
15. Configure the Product Registration/Line Setup. Use the Kompensation template for best results when using an encoder.
The Kompensation template will help to ensure good settings for Encoder Distance per Rotation, Angle of Laser, and Dir -
ection of Product Movement. See "Kompensation Template" on page 200 .
16. Adjust Parameter settings to get a good mark quality using the customer production line running at production speed.
17. Configure the IP address for Ethernet port (s), if necessary. You can set two IP addresses through the Web Interface (see
"Edit Laser Configuration" on page 323 ).
Safety Circuit
The 7230/7330 laser comes standard with a Safety Power Module (SPM) circuit board that is the center of the safety system in
the laser. If a Performance Level of D (shutterlock) or E (interlock) is required, then order the laser with the optional CPD3 and
FASS board. It is possible to achieve a Performance Level of D for interlock with the standard SPM board, but only when a spe -
cific switch and are used. Any deviation from the specified parts will result in no Performance Level rating.
SPM only (included) provides:
l Dual channel Shutterlock circuit (no Performance Level rating)
l Dual/redundant Emergency circuit complaint to performance level D
l Functions as an interlock with manual reset.
l Is Performance Level D only when using specified parts and wiring
l Dual channel Interlock circuit with or without manual reset switch.
l No Performance Level rating
l Must use mechanical switches/relays instead of solid state
l Customer must supply a reset switch
CPD3 board together with FASS board (upgrade) provides:
l Dual channel redundant door circuit compliant to performance level D (functions as Shutterlock)
l Door circuit extension
l Dual/redundant Emergency circuit complaint to performance level E (functions as Interlock with manual reset)
l Emergency circuit extension
l Manual emergency reset
l Customer must supply a reset switch
Both the SPM and CPD3 are designed to be used with double- pole switches as two circuits are monitored.
The boards are easily distinguished visually by their color. The SPM board is white and the CPD3 is green. You can see the SPM
board or CPD3 board and its X9 connector through the cutout in the mounting plate between the Customer Interface section of
Chameleon board and the rear panel of supply unit.
190
Rev AA

Installation
Expected Behavior: Interlock
Use an interlock in Mark on the Fly applications where doors are only opened to fix issues or clear product jams.
1. Shutter closes
2. System powers off supply to laser
3. Emission LED OFF
4. System reports “Interlock Open”
5. Close door/contact
- 5 second delay between door close and laser power on again
With manual reset switch
- Requires reset signal before can continue
6. System reports "Laser Ready"
Shutterlock
Use shutterlock in applications where the door will be opened/closed frequently.
1. Shutter closes
2. Laser source remains powered
3. System reports "Shutterlock Open"
4. Emission LED is ON
5. Resets when the door is closed (no reset signal necessary)
SPM/SPM- 16A Wiring
There are three ways to wire the SPM (7230/7330 models) or SPM- 16A (3640 model) to interlock or shutterlock system. The
method used is determined by the desired function. If laser model has an SPM- 16- A- FASS or a CPD board, please refer to the sec -
tions specific to those boards.
The following options can be achieved with the SPM/SPM- 16A board:
l Shutterlock (no Performance Level rating)
l Interlock with or without manual reset switch (no Performance Level rating)
l Emergency circuit with manual reset switch (compliant to Performance Level D)
Use Only Specified Parts
In order to avoid damage to components or to achieve stated Performance Level rating, it is important to use
the parts specified in the following wiring descriptions. Use of any parts other than those specified will result in
either no Performance Level rating or in damage to contacts of switches used for the interlock.
191
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Shutterlock
For shutterlock functionality (mirrors go to shutter closed position, but laser is still powered), on connector X9 of the SPM/SPM-
16A remove the jumper on pins X9.5 to X9.16 and the jumper on X9.7 to X9.14. Connect double pole switch to pins 5 and 16
(shutterlock circuit 2) and pins 7 and 14 (shutterlock circuit 1). No reset switch is required for shutterlock function. Leave all other
jumpers in place.
When wired as a Shutterlock system, behavior is as follows when the door is opened:
1. Shutterlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to the shutter closed position
3. Laser tube remains powered and capable of emitting radiation
4. Red emissions LEDs remain on
5. Must press Start to restart the marking process.
Interlock (No Performance Level)
To wire interlock with no Performance Level rating, remove jumpers 15- 19 and 17- 21 on connector X9 of SPM/SPM- 16A board.
Connect one circuit of double pole mechanical switch to pins 15 and 19 and other circuit to pins 17 and 21. Use of paired
screened cable is highly recommended. It is also recommended to create shield/ground connection on grounding bar (T shaped
cutouts in sheet metal between X9 and rear of supply unit cabinet). The grounding bar can also be used as strain relief for cables
by using cable ties to the T shapes.
Possible Equipment Damage - Mechanical Switches Only
Due to the high current used to close the relays on the SPM- 16A board, you must use mechanical switches
(never solid state or reed switches) to wire the interlock. If non mechanical switches are used, the current will
damage the switch contacts.
Must use mechanical, not solid state or reed switches, to wire interlock due to the high current that is used to
close the relays on the SPM- 16A board. Damage will occur to contacts in solid state or reed switches if used.
Using a manual reset switch is optional when no performance level is needed. To install a manual reset switch:
1. Remove the jumper from pins 1- 23 on X9 connector.
2. Connect a push button (mushroom button) style switch between pins 1- 23.
It is necessary to use a switch that pops back up on its own for the reset.If a toggle or other switch that stays closed
when depressed is used is in the closed position when laser is booted a fatal error will occur.
Behavior when wired for Interlock (with or without Performance Level):
1. Interlock Open status appears in SmartGraph or other interface
2. Mirrors go to shutter closed position
3. Laser tube powered down
4. Red emissions LEDs go off
192
Rev AA

Installation
5. If using manual reset switch, reset button must be pushed and released or Interlock Open status continues
6. Must press Start to restart the marking process
Emergency Circuit with Manual Reset Switch
In order to achieve Performance Level D, the following instructions and specified parts must be used or no Performance Level rat -
ing will be reached. Deviation of any sort will result in no Performance Level rating.
l You must use Schmersal AZ- 16- 02ZVRK (minimum contact load 12 V / 5 mA) switch. You will also need to order an actu -
ator for this part based on the physical setup of safety enclosure. Videojet does not carry these parts.
l Use paired, screened (shielded) cable.
l Install a reset switch (push button or mushroom button; must pop back up after being depressed).
l You must use 24V_ fused output on X9 for Reset Switch to maintain Level D rating and to avoid errors causing an Interlock
Open status even when closed.
Wiring Diagram
on page 191 shows wiring for both an interlock and a shutterlock. Typically only one or the other will be used. Which is used
depends on desired behavior.
l If wiring for an interlock , leave Shutterlock jumpers (X9.5 to X9.16 and X9.7 to X9.14) in place.
l If wiring for a Shutterlock , leave Interlock jumpers (X9.15 to X9.19 and X9.17 to X9.21) in place.
193
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 7- 1: Diagram: Wiring for an interlock or shutterlock (7230/7330)

Installation
Safety System Installation - CPD and FASS
The following descriptions pertain to installation of Shutterlock, Interlock, and/or Emergency Switch on units with FASS board
installed in addition to the CPD.
The following is the expected behavior with CPD+FASS:
l Shutterlock - Shutter closes, but laser power remains on. No reset from Emergency Stop Reset required.
l Interlock - Shutter closes and laser is powered off. Requires reset from Emergency Stop Reset (S02)
Safety System Selection
Always select the correct safety system configuration in the laser Web Interface.
For instructions on viewing or editing the safety system settings see "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 323 .
Connecting to CPD X9 (Cable Glands)
Connection to the safety system is accomplished through the CPD board X9 connector. Two variations are shown in Figure 7- 2
on the next page .
l Shutterlock (1): Connect door switch 1 to pins 7 and 14 and door switch 2 to pins 5 and 16. Leave all other jumpers in place.
l Interlock (2): Emergency switch 1 to pins 17 and 21 and Emergency switch 2 to pins 15 and 19 AND Emergency Switch
Reset to pins 1 - 23. Leave all other jumpers in place. Emergency Switch Reset should always be a push button switch that is
open except to reset after doors are opened and closed.
195
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 7- 2: Diagram: Connecting to CPD X9 (Cable Glands).
Table 7- 1 on the facing page shows the jumpers fitted on CPD board X9 connector by default from the factory. If using the Shut -
terlock functionality, leave the Interlock (Emergency) jumpers in place. If using the Interlock functionality, leave the Shutterlock
jumpers in place.
196
Rev AA

Installation
Table 7- 1: Default CPD X9 Jumper Positions
Default Jumpers (X9) Use
10- 12 Door Feedback
22- 24 Emergency Feedback
5- 16 Door 2 (Shutterlock)
7- 14 Door 1 (Shutterlock)
15- 19 Emergency 2 (Interlock)
17- 21 Emergency 1 (Interlock)
Connecting to CPD X9 (Quick Connectors)
When connecting emergency and door switches to the laser unit with rear panel quick connectors, you must use a 7- Pin DIN Con -
nector and Cable to connect switches rather than connecting directly to the CPD board X9 connector.
It is not recommended to use the Quick Connector panel when using CPD + FASS in Interlock mode. Wiring as Interlock requires
a reset signal and the 7 pin cable/connector does not have enough wires to do this easily.
If using CPD and FASS in Shutterlock mode, then it is necessary to reassign wires from the Customer Interface on the CPD3 to the
rear panel connector inside the supply unit as shown in Table 7- 2 below .

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Kompensation Template
The purpose of the Kompensation Test is to fine tune the Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between the Laser and Direction of
Product Movement in the Product Registration. The test is highly recommended for applications involving a 2D data matrix bar
code, but should be used in most installations to achieve most accurate results on setup of encoder.
The following procedure is described using the SmartGraph software.
Importing the Kompensation Template
1. Download the Kompensation.tpl template to a location on your computer.
You can find the Kompensation template file in the VTI Support & Technology team space.
2. Launch SmartGraph.
3. Connect to the Laser in the File tab. Ensure that after connection, the Laser side (right hand side) of the File tab is high -
lighted. The highlighted side is where the imported file will be saved.
4. Click Import .
5. Select the Import File option from the drop- down menu.
6. Browse to the Kompensation template on your computer. Ensure that the Type of File in the bottom of the window is set
to look for Templates (*.tpl).
200
Rev AA

Installation
7. Select the template and click Open .
8. Verify that the Kompensation template now appears in the list of Templates on the right hand side of screen in the File
tab.
Setting the Product Registration
1. Ensure that the Laser side of File tab is active, indicated by an orange banner above the files section.
2. Click the Configuration tab.
3. Click the Marking Head tab.
4. Set the Marking Head orientation based on the orientation of the marking head relative to the product. A small picture
will show the orientation of the head based on your choice. Adjust until the orientation matches the mounting of the laser.
5. Click Apply .
6. Click the Product Registration tab .
201
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
7. Click New to create a new product registration.
8. Name your file and click OK.
9. Make the necessary adjustment to your product registration base on Trigger type, Start Distance, and Blocking.
10. Enter twice the pulses per rotation of your encoder. For example, if a 2000 pulse per rotation encoder enter 4000 pulses in
the Product Registration.
11. Enter the diameter of encoder wheel (if present) in the Pulses Per Rotation box.
12. Enter the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement . Use the chart below to assist in assessing the set -
ting for both Mounting Position and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement (abbreviated Product
Movement Angle).
202
Rev AA

Installation
Setting the Parameters
The Kompensation template imports with a Parameter set called AP08111318.
Product Movement Too Fast Faults
Depending on the conveyor line speed you may have to adjust the parameters to avoid Product Movement
Too Fast faults.
203
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Adjust the Marking Speed to a speed fast enough to avoid Product Movement Too Fast errors, but slow enough to create a mark
on the product.
Printing the Kompensation Template
1. Click on the Marking tab.
2. Click on the Marking Adjustment tab.
3. Select the Kompensation template from the drop- down list.
4. Click on the Marking tab.
204
Rev AA

Installation
5. Press the Start button.
6. The laser status should indicate Waiting for Trigger. If this is not displayed, check that you have permissions to fire the
laser and that the Trigger Enable input has 24 volts applied (often jumpered X25.9- X25.12).
See "Permissions" on page 356 .
7. As the product passes through the product detector you should see the Marking indication. If not, adjust the Start
Distance in your Product Registration.
Troubleshooting
To center the product in the print area adjust the Start Delay in Product Registration.
If message is unreadable check the Angle of Product Movement in Product Registration.
If message is of correct orientation, but of poor quality, adjust the Parameters.
Understanding the Results
If both the Distance Per Rotation in encoder setting and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are correct
then the mark from the Kompensation template should be a cross with no curves on either axis as shown in Figure 7- 4 below .
F IGURE 7- 4: A mark indicating correct Distance Per Rotation and Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement are
both correct.
Make adjustments to correct for curvature in the horizontal or vertical axis.
205
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Setting the Correct Mounting Position
Once the correct Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement is found it may be necessary to
adjust the Mounting Position in the Marking Head tab. If conveyor line direction is left to right then the Mount -
ing Position should be adjusted to the same number as the Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product
Movement. If conveyor direction is right to left then Mounting position should be adjusted to 180 degree off
from Angle Between Laser and Direction of Product Movement.
206
Rev AA

Installation
F IGURE 7- 5: Kompensation Template troubleshooting tips.
207
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
SG Installation Lab
In this lab you will install a 7230/7330 including hardware connection, safety system, and product registration using a conveyor.
Complete the following tasks:
1. Connect encoder, product detect, and switch for interlock.
2. Adjust Mounting Position to correct head orientation.
3. Create Product Registration called SG Install with the following attributes:
l Fires once when Start is pressed and then stops firing
l No product movement.
4. Ensure Working Distance is correct for your head/lens combination. What tab in SmartGraph shows the lens focal dis -
tance and the working distance.
Tab (Choose One): File / Marking / Template / Configuration
Subtab: _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _
5. Mark Message 1 in static mode using your product registration. Adjust parameters for good mark quality.
6. Import Kompensation Template.
7. Attach your SG Install Product Registration to the Kompensation template.
8. Change the settings in your Product Registration to: mark once on each product using conveyor.
9. Adjust Parameters and Product Registration where necessary to ensure Kompensation image is straight along both axes
and mark is of good quality with no faults.
10. Create a text message and mark using your Product Registration to verify setup of text code is acceptable.
11. Verify that Interlock behaves as expected (when open message reads Interlock Open and emissions LED is off)
208
Rev AA

Maintenance
This chapter provides an overview of the preventative maintenance recommended for the 7230/7330 laser system.
Maintenance Schedule
The 7230/7330 laser system requires a relatively low amount of maintenance, but the environment in which the laser operates
can significantly affect the maintenance interval.
Possible Injury or Equipment Damage
Only Videojet trained personnel should carry out the installation and maintenance work. Any such work under -
taken by unauthorized personnel can damage the printer and invalidate the warranty.
Personal Injury
Before the beginning of the cleaning work of the laser system and its environment, the laser system must be
disconnected from the power supply.
Symptoms indicating maintenance is needed include:
l Faint marking (dirty lens)
l Overheating faults (beam source)
Table 8- 1: Maintenance Interval & Tasks
Interval Maintenance Tasks
Monthly l Check the laser scan lens for dust and dirt.
l Clean the laser scan lens in case of dust or stains.
Monthly or when con - l Exchange the filter bag (if installed) inside the fume extractor (refer to
trol lamp illuminates manual of the fume extractor).
l If the Fume Extractor Filter Full control lamp illuminates, exchange the
large particle filter (if installed). Refer to the fume extractor manual for
details.
209
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Interval Maintenance Tasks
Quarterly or more fre - l Visually inspect the laser system and clean if necessary.
quently in dirty ambi -
ent conditions
Six Months l Create a CMark backup. Create a separate backup of the database
(user interface) more frequently if jobs/templates are created often. See
"CMark Backup" on page 332 for procedure.
l Exchange the charcoal filter (if installed) inside the fume extractor. Refer
to the fume extractor manual for details.
Lens Cleaning Procedure
Read before carrying out maintenance work at the optics
Before carrying out the maintenance works at the optics, please note the following:
l Acetone is not included in delivery and has to be purchased via other companies. See www.vwr.com to order
acetone in a quick and uncomplicated way.
l When purchasing acetone, make sure that you order acetone pro analysis (p.a. = highest purity grade).
Acetone is preferred over Isopropyl alcohol.
The laser scan lens is located at the marking head. It may become contaminated by dust or floating particles. Contamination of
the laser scan lens can lead to damage of the laser scan lens and will gradually reduce the quality of the marking. You should
therefore clean the lens regularly.
Generally just the outwardly facing side of the laser scan lens requires cleaning, but check both sides for contamination and
clean as necessary.
Personal Injury - Zinc Selenide
The laser scan lens consists of coated zinc selenide. This material is hazardous. Avoid contact and take care to
not damage the lens, which may release the zinc selenide as a particulate.
To prevent personal injury when handling these substances, always wear protective clothing and rubber gloves.
In case of contact with the laser scan lens with the skin, wash the respective part of skin with water and soap
immediately. Avoid any surface scratches of the laser scan lens. Do not inhale material particles. In the event
of a broken laser scan lens, carefully pack the fragments into a closed plastic bag and return to for disposal.
210
Rev AA

Maintenance
Lens Damage
As with all optical components the laser scan lens is an object of highest precision and sophisticated design!
Slightest damages of the surface might (in the long term) result in unusability of the component or reduction
of marking quality.
Avoid any surface scratches of the laser scan lens.Remove any contaminants using an optics cleaning cloth
and isopropyl alcohol or acetone (included in the accessory box).
Ensure that no contaminants enter the marking head while cleaning the laser scan lens!
Required Supplies
l Optics cleaning paper
l Acetone (methanol or isopropyl alcohol also acceptable)
l Protective gloves
Clean the Scan Lens
Personal Injury - Shock Hazard Warning
Before starting any work the laser system must be dead!
Personal InjuryPersonal Injury
To prevent personal injury when handling these substances, always wear protective clothing and rubber gloves.
1. Turn the key switch to position »0«.
2. Remove the key to ensure that the laser system cannot be switched on.
3. Switch off the main switch.
4. Disconnect the mains connector.
5. Remove the Marking Head. Note that the mounting of lenses for each model differ slightly.
Possible Equipment Damage
Do not touch the lens surface!
SHF60A/SHF60B Marking Heads
The lens is in a holder with a bayonet lock. The bayonet lock is unlocked after a quarter turn.
211
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
5.1. Carefully rotate the laser scan lens anti- clockwise by a quarter turn. Do not touch the surface of the laser scan lens with
the gloves!
5.2. Remove the laser scan lens from the marking head and place it on a clean surface.
SHF100A/SHF100B Marking Heads
5.1. Firmly grip the laser scan lens with both hands and turn it carefully until it is released from the thread.
Hold the lens with both hands
The fine thread requires several turns before the scan lens is released. Make sure that you are hold -
ing the lens firmly with both hands.
5.2. Remove the laser scan lens from the marking head and place it on a clean surface.
6. Place the laser scan lens onto a clean surface.
Avoid damage to the lens
l Never use polishing agents on the laser lens. Polishing agents will destroy the laser scan lens.
l Never use contaminated optics cleaning paper.
l Make sure that the optics cleaning paper is stored in a clean place.
Fold a sheet of optics cleaning paper at least 5 times to create a flat cushion.
7. Hold the optics cleaning paper between thumb and index finger and put a few drops of acetone onto the optics cleaning
paper.
212
Rev AA

Maintenance
8. Carefully and slowly pull the optics cleaning paper across the surface to be cleaned working from center to outer edge.
l Make sure that you do no touch the surface with gloves!
l Apply only very light pressure onto the paper!
9. If necessary repeat the procedure with a new sheet of optics cleaning paper.
10. Check the surface of the lens side located in the marking head for contamination. If necessary, clean the inner side of
lens as described above.
213
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Complete the First Steps section first when troubleshooting marking issues or performance problems with a laser system.
First Steps
1. Clean the output lens.
See "Lens Cleaning Procedure" on page 210 .
2. Ensure that the Working Distance is correct for head/lens combination (marking head to product distance).
See "Working Distance" on page 137
3. Check that the product detector and encoder are connected and in serviceable condition.
4. Ensure the umbilical and other cables are in serviceable condition.
5. Power the laser ON.
l Look up any Error messages
6. Check the Product Registration/Line Setup.
l Verify that the desired Product Registration is assigned to Template/Job
l Verify that the settings in the Product Registration are correct for the application
l Verify the Configuration settings (mark head type, lens type) are correct for the installed components.
7. Fire in static mode to decide if the laser is capable of marking when no peripherals are involved.
To static fire:
l If using SmartGraph, set Product Registration to No Product Movement and set Triggers to Auto and None
l If using TCS or Clarity, set Line Setup to Static Product Movement and Internal Once Trigger
Error & Warning Categories
The type of Warning or Error detected by the laser system determines the behavior of the laser. Following are the types of warn -
ings and errors and the behavior of the laser system when detected.
215
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Table 9- 1: Error & Warning Types with Category Symbols
Type Category
Fatal Error 0 Requires system to be rebooted
System Error 1 Power supply to laser turned off
Error 2 Marking is stopped
Warning W System continues to mark
Warnings
l Marking may be continued with Warning conditions.
Errors
l Yellow LED  on supply unit will flash.
l System Errors (1) and Errors (2) must be confirmed in user interface before marking can be restarted.
l Fatal Errors (0) cannot be confirmed and system must be restarted before marking can be restarted.
l With all error classes the condition causing the error must be corrected or error will reoccur.
216

Troubleshooting
Troubleshooting - No Power
Symptom: No light on Power/Standby button on the membrane keyboard.
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply.
1. Ensure the Laser system is plugged into mains power.
2. Check whether the green 48V Present LED  on the Chameleon board is illuminated.
3. Continue troubleshooting based on the state of the LED.
48V Present LED Is Not Illuminated
1. If the 48V Present LED  is not illuminated, measure voltage at the Chameleon board to verify 48V at X28.11 (48V) and
X.28.10 (Ground).
Recently Removed Mounting Plate
If work was recently completed that involved removing the mounting plate inside the supply unit prior
to loss of power, verify wiring at X28.
2. If no 48V, disconnect the system from mains power and check the two fuses in the rear of the supply unit.
3. If no 48V, check the mains power cord and try an alternative power cord.
4. If no 48V, check the output of the 48V power supply at the power supply. Possible defective parts include the 48V power
supply, the SPM or CPD3, or the internal harness.
48V Present LED Is Illuminated
If the 48V LED Present LED is illuminated on the Chameleon board but the blue/white light on the keypad is not, then the mem -
brane keypad and membrane keypad cable harness are suspect.
1. Disconnect the system from mains power.
2. Bridge pins 3 and 5 on X28 and connect main power.
3. If 48V green LED lights after power connected and system boots, replace the membrane keypad cable harness.
4. Boot without pins 3 and 5 bridged to check for normal operation.
5. If power is still not present, replace the membrane keypad.
281
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Change Fuses
There are two fuses located in the power cord receptacle at the rear of the supply unit. The primary symptom of fuse failure is an
inability to power on the laser unit. Fuses are present in both the SPM and CPD3 boards.
Remove Fuse Insert
Shock Hazard Warning
Lethal voltages are present within this equipment when it is connected to the mains electrical supply. Before
starting any work power the laser system OFF and completely disconnected from mains power supply.
1. Remove the power cord from the power receptacle on the laser unit.
2. Pinch the tabs on both sides of the fuse insert (see Figure 9- 3 below ) and pull out the insert.
F IGURE 9- 3: Removing the fuse insert.
3. Remove the fuses and dispose of them according to the local regulations.
4. Insert new fuses (2 x T8A) into the insert.
282
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
5. Put the fuse insert back into the SPM/CPD.
6. Reinstall the power cord.
283
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Failed Boot - MCIP Chameleon
l Symptom: White light on Power/Standby button flashes as boot process starts, but does not stop flashing after 5 minutes or
more.
l Symptom: When the Power/Standby button is pressed to start the boot process, the white light comes on solid, but does not
start flashing.
Multi- Core Chameleon Diagnostics
1. Did you wait long enough? There is approximately 20 second lag time before white light starts to flash. Approximately 5
minutes after white light starts to flash before it remains on solid.
2. Is the battery attached to the Chameleon board? Does battery produce at least 2.7 volts (minimum voltage for boot)?
3. Is there 48V sent from Chameleon board to CP- Light?
l Measure at X28.12 (48V) and X28.6 (Ground) during boot. If there is no voltage at X28.12, verify wiring at X28. If wir -
ing is correct, replace the Chameleon board. If voltage is present at X28, then proceed to step 4.
l Measure 48V at power connector on CP- Light board during boot - If no voltage at CP- Light power connector replace
umbilical.
4. Unplug the laser system and insert a Rescue Stick USB drive with a rescue image file into USB port on Chameleon. This
image is more than just the CMark operating system.
l If corrupted files on the Chameleon board are the issue causing boot to fail, overwriting the image will allow the
laser to boot after the rescue process is complete. It is necessary to connect to the Web Interface and load
CMark through the Web Interface if the system boots successfully.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symp -
tom of corrupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is
inserted into the USB port and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from
memory and overwritten with the factory default image. All customer files and CMark will be
lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Marking Head type/Lens/Serial
Number/IP addresses).
l See "The Chameleon Rescue Stick" on the facing page for overview of Rescue Stick concept.
See "Creating a Rescue Stick" on the facing page for detailed instructions on creating a Rescue Stick.
See "Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a Rescue Stick" on page 288 for detailed instructions on overwriting sys -
tem with Rescue Stick.
5. If the laser fails to boot following use of Rescue Stick, replace the Chameleon board.
284
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
The Chameleon Rescue Stick
A Rescue Stick is a USB memory stick prepared with a CMark image that allows you to recover a failed laser. You can create a res -
cue stick and carry it with you as a service tool.
Minimum Required Version
CMark versions 4.0 and later support the Rescue Stick feature. You cannot recover single core Chameleon
boards with the rescue stick, only multi- core boards.
Use a Rescue Stick:
l When a laser running CMark from the Flash memory will not boot.
l When a laser will not finish the boot cycle, preventing you from reloading CMark via the Web Interface.
l Before replacing the Chameleon to resolve failure to boot. A Rescue Stick will overwrite system files on the Chameleon,
allowing the laser to successfully boot if corrupted system files were the root cause.
It is suggested that you create a rescue stick before you need it and carry it as service tool. It takes approximately 45 minutes to
download the rescue image and write the files to USB.
When a prepared Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB slot on a Chameleon board, it will automatically overwrite anything in the
Chameleon Board Flash Memory. This will completely erase all files on Chameleon and set it to the manufacturer default
state with the exception of the system database . After this rewrite process, it is necessary to connect to the Web Interface to
load CMark. CMark is not included as part of the rescue image. After loading CMark, you should verify the Laser Configuration in
the Web Interface.
Possible Data Loss - Full System Rewrite
Use a Rescue Stick only as a last resort if corruption of system files is suspected. The main symptom of cor -
rupted system files is an inability to boot the system successfully. If a Rescue Stick is inserted into the USB port
and the laser is booted, all existing customer files will be wiped from memory and overwritten with the factory
default image. All customer files and CMark will be lost. The system database is not erased (Laser type/Mark -
ing Head type/Lens/Serial Number/IP addresses).
Creating a Rescue Stick
The following procedure details installing the Rescue Image to a USB memory stick.
1. Download both CMark_ Rescue_ 1.x.img and HDD Raw Copy Tool.exe . Contact Videojet Technical Support if you do not
have access to the download locations.
285
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
VTI Technology & Support Team Space File Location
Service Resources/Service Software/Laser/Utility/Rescue Stick for Multicore Chameleon
2. Insert a USB stick with a capacity at least 2 GB into your PC.
3. Double click on the HDD Raw Copy Tool.exe file to launch the program.
4. Click on FILE  from the list of sources.
5. Browse to and select the Rescue Image file you downloaded.
286
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
6. Click Continue .
7. Select your USB device from the list.
USB File Overwrite
All files on selected USB drive will be overwritten!
8. Click Continue .
9. Click Start .
The Copy tab will show progress and display Task is Complete in the log when done.
10. Close Raw Copy Tool when complete.
11. Safely eject the USB device from your computer.
Your Rescue Stick is now usable to recover any failed laser running a Multi- Core Chameleon board .

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
See "Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a Rescue Stick" below for detailed instructions on using the rescue stick to recover a laser
system.
Recover Multi- Core Chameleon with a Rescue Stick
This process only applies to Multi- Core Chameleon boards that will not successfully boot. This procedure will not recover a single
core Chameleon. Recovery of single core Chameleon boards that will not boot should be attempted through reloading of the SD
card with CMark or replacement of the SD card.
Laser Will Be Set Back to Factory Image
When a laser system is booted with a Rescue Image USB stick inserted into the USB slot on the Chameleon
board, it will wipe all files on the laser's flash storage. Those files are then irretrievable. The desired CMark must
be available for installation after Rescue Image is installed in order for the laser to function correctly.
The System Database, which is stored on EEPROM on Chameleon, will not be lost. This contains the laser type,
head type, lens, serial number and other hardware configuration items.
1. Insert the USB stick with the Rescue Image into USB port on the Chameleon board of the laser. For instructions on transfer
of Rescue Image to USB see "Creating a Rescue Stick" on page 285 .
2. Ensure the laser system is connected to power before proceeding. The blue light on the membrane keypad should be lit.
3. Press the On/Standby button on the membrane keypad of the laser to boot the laser. The white light will come on dimly
and eventually start to flash as the image on the USB memory stick begins to load. The laser system will shut down when
the image load is complete. Approximate time to completion is 5- 10 minutes.
If the Rescue Image loading was successful, the laser system should boot when powered on and will allow you to con -
nect to the Web Interface to continue. You must load CMark before checking the system configuration .
The only part of system that is not overwritten is the EEPROM. The EEPROM stores the system configuration including IP
addresses, laser type, head type, lens type, and serial number. Even though the EEPROM is not overwritten, check
these for accuracy after loading CMark or CMark Backup.
Troubleshooting: Laser still won't boot
If the laser system still will not boot, replace the Chameleon board.
4. Install the desired CMark version or CMark Backup.
Installing a fresh install of CMark or a CMark backup are achieved through the same steps. For detailed information on
backup creation see "CMark Backup" on page 332 . Following are generalized steps for loading either CMark or a CMark
backup
Generalized steps to install CMark or CMark backup
4.1. Boot the laser.
4.2. Connect to the laser's Web Interface. Use Primary IP to connect (dip switch 1 in Off position). The IP previously set
288
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
by customer is not overwritten during the Rescue Stick process. If you are unsure of the primary IP Address, you can
connect to 192.168.1.1 with dip switch 1 in the On position to find the configured primary IP in the laser Web Inter -
face at Laser Menu | Configuration.
4.3. Set DIP Switch 1 to OFF if you used it to determine the primary IP Address.
4.4. Connect to the laser via the primary IP address.
Use Internet Explorer version 9 or newer only for CMark Update or any other file transfer.
The Web Interface branding will be Alltec Foba until after CMark is loaded.
4.5. Log in with the service level username and password.
289
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
4.6. Click on the Other menu and select CMark Update from the drop- down menu.
4.7. Browse to the desired CMark version or CMark Backup (if available) on your computer. After CMark or CMark
backup loads the laser will shut down.
If upgrading from a single core Chameleon you may insert the SD card from the single core board before selecting
the CMark version and inherit the Customer Database from the SD.
5. Reboot the laser.
6. Connect to the Web Interface and check the Laser Configuration for accuracy. Following are generalized steps.
For more detailed information see "Edit Laser Configuration" on page 323 .
Generalized steps for checking/setting laser configuration
6.1. Boot the laser system.
6.2. Connect to the laser's Web Interface. Web Interface is now Videojet branded since CMark update.
290
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
6.3. Log in with the service username and password.
6.4. Click to select the Laser menu.
6.5. Select Configure .
6.6. Check or set the appropriate Laser Type, Head Type, Lens Type, and CPD/SPM/FASS at a minimum. Errors will occur
if these hardware items are not set correctly. The serial number, name for laser, IP addresses, and other information
may also be configured in this window.
CMark must be loaded before Laser Types/Marking Head/Lens will be shown in the Configuration menu. If the Laser
Configuration group shows NO LASER FILE, NO MARKINGHEAD, NO LENS, and the page has
Alltec/FOBA branding, then repeat the CMark Update process.
6.7. Click Save . Reboot the laser when prompted.
6.8. Load the Customer Database backup (if available or if necessary).
If no CMark Backup has been loaded, or if a newer Customer Database is available than was included in the CMark
Backup, then the Customer Database may be loaded using the appropriate user interface. If no backups are avail -
able to be installed, proceed to the desired user interface to create jobs/templates for laser marking.
291

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Replacing Chameleon Board
The spare part Chameleon board does not come with the CMark operating system installed. You must load CMark and then set
the laser Configuration through the Web Interface before any other actions can be performed.
When a spare board arrives it is necessary to:
1. Move HIC and LIC from the bad board to new Chameleon board. The HIC and LIC do not come with Chameleon spare
part.
2. Boot and connect with the Web Interface. The Web Interface will display Alltec FOBA branding until after the CMark
Update.
3. Login with service username and password.
4. Upload CMark or CMark backup (Multi- Core Chameleon only) via the Web Interface.
5. After the CMark Update is complete, reboot the laser.
292
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
6. Login with service username and password.
7. Select Configuration in the Laser drop- down menu.
8. Set the Laser Type, Marking Head Type, Lens, Serial Number, and CPD/SPM/FASS configuration at a minimum. If backup
of System Database is available it may be loaded through Other | System Database .
9. Click Save button.
10. If a backup of the Customer Database is available, load through the available user interface.
293
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
No Communication with Marking Head
The following faults are considered marking head faults. The CP- Light board is neither transmitting nor receiving signals from
the galvos or the Chameleon board.
Symptom: Laser boots, but the following error occurs: No Communication with Marking Head error (E09100)
Diagnostics
1. Connect to the laser Interface and verify that the laser model and marking head type are set correctly.
See "Connect to the Web Interface" on page 321 for connection instructions.
If model and marking head type are correct continue to step 2.
If the model or marking head type are not correct:
1.1. Log in to the Web Interface
1.2. Change to the correct type.
1.3. Reboot the laser system after making the change.
2. Are the LEDs illuminated on the CP- Light board?
If the LEDs are illuminated, continue to step 3.
If no LEDs are not illuminated:
2.1. Check for 48V at the power connector on the CP- Light board.
2.2. If there is no voltage, verify 48V out at X28 pins 6 and 12.
3. Check the continuity on the LVDS cable (CAT- 7 cable) that runs between the HIC and the CP- Light 3.
4. Ensure the marking head cable is securely connected at the galvos.
5. Check the HIC board.
294
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Marking Head Temperature too High
Error
Laser boots successfully, but the following error occurs: Marking Head: Temp of FM/MHI Assembly Group Too High (E09000)
Diagnostics
1. Verify that the jumper is in place on the LIC board. This is a white, plastic jumper with a wire bridge from pins 1- 3 as
shown in Figure 9- 4 below .
F IGURE 9- 4: LIC board jumper.
2. Log in to the web interface and open the System Information page.
3. Check the Galvo Block Temperature value. The warning level for the galvo block is 62 degrees Celsius and a fault occurs
at 65 degrees Celsius.
4. If the temperature shown is above 65 degrees Celsius, try the following mitigations:
l Lower duty cycle by increasing distance between products
l Decrease jump speed
l Improve cooling of marking head
295
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Beam Shutter Errors
There is a mechanical shutter in the marking head of the laser system that must work or the laser system will fault. The shutter is
driven by voltage and signals from the HIC board and must return feedback to the HIC verifying an open or closed position.
Either of two faults can occur if the shutter is not functioning or the feedback regarding position is not received:
l E10302 - Shutter does not stay open
l E10303 - Shutter does not stay closed
Components
The following components are critical to the correct function of the shutter system:
l Chameleon
l HIC
l Marking Head Cable
l Shutter Assembly (includes the mechanical shutter and shutter board)
Diagnostics
1. Verify connection of marking head cable at HIC and at shutter board.
2. Verify continuity of shutter cable.
Table 9- 2: Shutter Cable Within Marking Head Cable
Shutter Board
X1 Connector HIC- CPA
X1
Pin Number Wire Color
Description
Description
1 +5 RED +5v
2 SHUTTER IS CLOSED WHITE SHUTTER IS CLOSED
3 PILOT LASER ON VIOLET PILOT LASER ON
4 EMISSION LED ON RED EMISSION LED ON
5 SHUTTER RETURN BROWN OUT SHUTTER
6 24V SHUTTER GREY IL RETURN
7 GND VIOLET GND
3. Replace the shutter if itis suspected that the shutter plate is binding.
296
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
X- or Y- Mirror Errors
There are several voltages and signals required to move the mirrors in the marking head. The galvos provide feedback to the CP-
Light3 to maintain a closed feedback loop. When either output voltage or signals are not returned to the CP- Light3 from the gal -
vos, the following errors will occur:
l Marking Head: Overpositioning of X- Mirror (E09002)
l Marking Head: Overpositioning of Y- Mirror (E09003)
l Marking Head: Overcurrent X- Mirror (E09004)
l Marking Head: Overcurrent Y- Mirror (E09005)
l No X Mirror Connected (E09006)
l No Y Mirror Connected (E09007)
Diagnostics
Possible Equipment Damage
When troubleshooting, only move galvos by hand when the system is in standby mode (blue light on keypad)
or when power to the system is completely OFF!
1. Verify X and Y galvo voltage connectors from CP- Light3 out to galvos are seated correctly.
2. Verify that the X and Y galvo position detect connectors (these carry feedback from galvos) are seated correctly on the CP-
Light3.
3. Verify that the wires at X28.1 (red) and X28.2 (black) are making good contact and are seated correctly.
297
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
4. Verify that the Galvo Release connection is in place on the CP- Light.
5. Verify galvo movement upon boot.
5.1. Place the laser in standby or power off.
5.2. Gently move the galvos out of home position.
5.3. Watching the galvos, startup the laser and observe if they return to the home position.
6. If the galvos do not move on startup, replace marking head cable.
298
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Red Emissions LED Defective Error
There are two red Emissions lights, one on the membrane keypad of the supply unit and one on the front of the marking head.
Each must be functional, or the system will not enable laser power.
l There are four LEDs embedded in the Emissions LED board in the marking head and all must be functional for the system
to operate.
l If any of the red emissions LEDs are not functioning, the following error will be displayed in the user interface: Red laser
emission LED defective (E00210)
Diagnostics
1. Check forward and backward resistance on the membrane keypad pins 2- 15. If open, replace the keypad.
2. Check the resistance between leads on the Emissions LED board. If open, replace the Emissions LED board.
299
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Wrong HIC Detected Error
Symptom: Wrong HIC Detected Error (E00213) after boot.
Diagnostics
1. Ensure the HIC board is present and securely connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Connect to Web Interface and verify that model and marking head type are correct.
3. If the model and marking head set in the Web Interface are not correct, log in and change Configuration to the correct
laser type or marking head type.
This is a Fatal Error, so a complete reboot of system is required in order to clear error.
300
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Wrong LIC Detected Error
Symptom: Wrong LIC detected error (E00212)
Diagnostics
1. Verify that the LIC is connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Connect to the Web Interface and select Laser | Configuration .
3. Verify that the Model Number is correct.
If the Model Number value is incorrect:
3.1. Log in to the Web Interface .
3.2. Select Laser | Configuration .
3.3. Choose the correct model number from the dropdown selector.
4. Check if the error is cleared. If not, continue troubleshooting.
5. Verify that both ribbon cables from the LIC are securely connected to the laser source. Note that you must remove the laser
source in order to access these connectors.
301
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Laser Not Ready Error
Symptom: Laser Not Ready Error (E00200)
Diagnostics
1. Connect to the Web Interface and go to the System Information page.
2. Ensure the key switch is in the Enabled position.
l If the Housekeeping Alarm shows a number other than 0 – Check the laser power supply.
l If the Temperature Alarm shows a number other than 0– Check for dust on laser source and fans.
F IGURE 9- 5: Web Interface System Information.
3. Check the serial connection (small ribbon cable) to the laser source. Upon key switch turn, the laser looks for info from
this source immediately.
Note Alarms if Source is Changed
If the laser source is replaced, document the number of alarms shown in the System Information sec -
tion as shown Figure 9- 5 above . Include this information when returning the defective source.
