Troubleshooting
General Error
Symptom: General Error (E00204)
Diagnostics
"General Error" is often a sign of a bad laser source, but check the following before replacing the laser source:
1. 24V at Terminal Block and back of laser source
2. Serial interface cable securely connected at back of laser source (small ribbon cable)
3. Digital interface cable securely connected at back of laser source (large ribbon cable)
303
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Defective Element in Laser Supply
Error
The system must sense 24 Volts from the laser power supply at three places. Voltage from the laser power supply must be
returned to the X28 connector on the Chameleon board and the X9 connector on the SPM or CPD board. If voltage is not
returned to X28 and X9, the following fault will occur: Defective Element in Laser Supply (E00211). If voltage is not sensed at
laser source then Laser Supply Voltage Too Low/High will occur.
Diagnostics
1. Verify that X9 pin 3 has the red wire (laser power) inserted completely and that X9 pin 13 has the gray (laser ground) inser -
ted completely.
2. Verify 24V is present to X9 pin 3 and ground at X9 pin 13. These wires can be difficult to seat because of their thickness. If
no voltage is read, try reinserting the wires.
304
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Interlock Open - Troubleshooting
The laser system uses a safety circuit that requires two circuits for the Interlock system to both be closed in order to enable the
laser power. If one of these two circuits is open, the Interlock Open condition cannot be cleared.
Symptom: Interlock Open status in user interface and no red LEDs on marking head or supply unit keypad
Diagnostics
1. Verify both interlock circuits are closed.
2. Check the wiring at the door switch/emergency switch on the customer side (no loose wires).
3. Verify the connection at X9 pins 17- 21 and 15- 19.
4. Check the fuse at X9.
5. If no FASS is installed:
5.1. Verify that the jumper from X9 pins 1- 23 is in place or that a reset switch is installed.
5.2. If a reset switch is installed, ensure that it is normally open.
5.3. If a reset switch is installed, ensure that it closes and opens following reset.
If a FASS is installed:
5.1. Ensure the reset switch is properly connected to connector X9 on pins 1- 23.
5.2. Ensure the reset switch is normally open.
5.3. Ensure the switch closes and opens again following interlock open state.
6. Verify that both interlock circuits open and close at same time. Too much time between open and close on circuit 1 and 2
results in the Interlock Open warning. A double pole switch is always recommended.
7. Verify SPM/CPD functionality:
7.1. Power down the laser.
7.2. Remove the customer wiring to X9 on the SPM/CPD board.
7.3. Install jumpers to the X9 as described in Table 9- 3 below .
7.4. Power on the laser and check if the Interlock Open warning has cleared.
7.5. If Interlock Open does not clear with the jumpers in place, replace the SPM board or CPD3 board.
Table 9- 3: Jumper Config to test SPM/CPD
Jumper Pins Signal
5- 16 Door 2 (Shutterlock)
305
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Jumper Pins Signal
7- 14 Door 1 (Shutterlock)
10- 12 Door Feedback
15- 19 Emergency 2 (Interlock)
17- 21 Emergency 1 (Interlock)
22- 24 Emergency Feedback
1- 23 Interlock Release - SPM Only (Not used with CPD+FASS)
306
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Clicking Noise When Interlock Is Open
The laser system opens and closes two relays on the SPM/CPD when any doors connected to the two interlock circuits are open.
These two circuits are meant to be redundant and to be used with double pole switch. When only one circuit is used and the other
circuit has jumper left in place, one of the relays struggles to stay closed. Over time, this will eventually damage the board
Symptom: Rhythmic clicking noise can be heard from supply unit while Interlock Open.
Solution
If using a single pole switch, use the wire the switch to X9 as shown in Figure 9- 6 below to avoid long term damage.
F IGURE 9- 6: Wiring for a single pole switch to XP on the SPM/CPD board.
On the X9 connector of SPM/CPD:
1. Remove the jumper from 15- 19.
2. Wire the single pole switch between pins 15 and 19 .
3. Remove the default jumper from 17- 21.
4. Place a jumper from 15- 17.
5. Place a jumper from 19- 21.
307
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
MIC Not Found Error
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
When installed, the MIC board must be enabled through the Web Interface. If the Web Interface settings do not match the pres -
ence of the MIC, then errors will occur.
Symptom: The Miscellaneous Interface Controller (MIC) was not found or is not supported. (E15100).
Diagnostics
l Verify the MIC board connected to Chameleon if it installed in the system.
l Verify that Web Interface | Other | External Interface is toggled to Disabled if no MIC is installed.
308
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Wrong MIC Detected Errors
Laser systems equipped with the Chameleon MCIP can accept an additional board to support EtherNet/IP or PROFINET with a
PLC for a limited set of functions. The MIC board contains the programming and the ports that must be used for EtherNet/IP for
PROFINET.
There are two different versions of MIC board. There is one MIC board for EtherNet/IP and one MIC board for PROFINET. The
boards contain the firmware specific to that version and this cannot be changed in the field.
Symptoms
l The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. EtherNet/IP module not found. (E15101)
l The Interface communications module couldn’t be detected. PROFINET module not found. (E15102)
Diagnostics
1. Verify the MIC board is connected to the Chameleon board.
2. Ensure that the correct MIC is installed (Blue dot for EtherNet/IP or Yellow for PROFINET).
3. In the Web Interface, select Other and verify that Interface is set to the correct MIC option (EtherNet/IP or PROFINET)
for the installed MIC.
309
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
SmartGraph Cannot Connect To Laser
Symptom: When attempting to connect to the laser using SmartGraph software, you receive the error, "Cannot Connect to Laser".
1. Verify that the laptop/PC IP address is set to work with the IP address of the laser system port. The first three octets of
the laptop/PC address must match that of the laser and the last octet must be different than that of the laser. Example:
The laser ACC board IP address is 192.168.1.1 so then the PC IP address must be 192.168.1._ _ (number 0- 255 other
than "1").
If the IP address of the laser is unknown:
1.1. Move DIP switch 1 to the ON position.
1.2. Restart the laser system. The laser IP will now be set to default IP of 192.168.1.1.
1.3. Set your PC to use an IP address compatible with 192.168.1.1.
2. Connect to the Web Interface. If you can connect to the Web Interface, a connection from SmartGraph is possible and
the issue is likely a version conflict between the SmartGraph version installed on your PC and the CMark version installed
on the laser.
See "Connect to the Web Interface" on page 321 for directions on connecting.
3. Verify the version of CMark installed on laser and its required version of SmartGraph needed:
3.1. Connect to the laser Web Interface.
3.2. Select Log | Boot Log .
3.3. Determine the version of SmartGraph required.
3.4. Ensure you have installed the correct version on your PC. See "Check Software Versions - Smartgraph & CMark" on
page 313 for directions on connecting to Web Interface and viewing versions.
4. If you still cannot connect, then the issue is with the connection itself.
4.1. Verify the Ethernet cable is in serviceable condition. You should be using a crossover cable, however most modern
PC/Laptop network adapters can auto switch to crossover mode for you.
4.2. Check for activity at the Ethernet status lights on Ethernet ports on Chameleon board.
4.3. Verify that the PC IP address is set on the correct network connection.
4.4. Boot the laser and connect Ethernet cable.
4.5. On the PC, select Start | Control Panel | Network and Sharing Center | Change Adapter Settings.
4.6. Look for the LAN connection that shows "Unidentified Network".
4.7. Unplug the Ethernet cable to the laser and verify the status changes to "Network Cable Unplugged."
4.8. If you see the status change for a different LAN connection, switch your troubleshooting to that connection.
310
Rev AA

Troubleshooting
Waiting for Trigger Status
Symptom: Laser status displays "Waiting for Trigger" and no mark is made.
Diagnostics
1. Verify the Start button has been pressed.
2. Check that interlocks are closed.
3. Ensure that the correct Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity) is selected.
4. Verify that the Trigger Enable input X25.9 has 24V applied or is jumpered to X25.12. Voltage is necessary to X25.9 for
trigger signals to be acknowledged.
Web Interface Set to Trigger Enable Voltage Needed
If Trigger Enable Voltage needed is set to Default (3x40) or to True and 24V is not applied to X25.9
then Waiting for Trigger status will appear and no mark will be allowed. This condition applies when
using Product Registration/Line Setup is set to an external source. Ensure that 24V is applied to X25.9
Trigger Enable input in order to avoid constant Waiting for Trigger condition.
5. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag product detector. If LED does not flash verify wiring and
exchange product detector.
311
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
No Mark on Product
Symptom: Product and Mark Count increase, but no mark is observed on product.
Diagnostics
1. Clean the output lens.
2. Verify working distance is correct for the lens fitted to marking head.
3. Adjust marking head so that it is perpendicular to product.
4. Remove any obstructions in beam path such as conveyor guards or fume extraction nozzles.
5. Verify Parameter settings are correct. Increase Intensity setting.
6. Verify Product Registration (SmartGraph)/Line Setup (TCS/Clarity Laser Controller). Are correct Trigger source and Start
Delay selected?
7. Attempt to mark with no product movement. Change product registration to one with No Product Movement and First
Trigger set to Auto and Consecutive Trigger set to None (SmartGraph) or Internal Once (TCS/Clarity). If laser marks
product with no product movement and internal trigger source then laser is capable of marking and problem is with
Product Registration or Parameters.
Attach Product Registration in SmartGraph
Ensure that after creating a Product Registration in SmartGraph you also attach the Product Regis -
tration to the Template in the Template tab. Changes to a Product Registration only take effect with a
Template if attached through Template tab.
8. Verify D20 LED (Trig Connected) on Chameleon is lit if using product detect. LED is lit if Product Detector is sensed by
Chameleon. System report error if Trigger Rising/Falling (SmartGraph) or External Rising/External Falling (TCS/Clarity) is
selected in user interface. Voltage and Ground for product detector must be attached to X25.4 (Voltage) and X25.8
(ground) or No Trigger Detector Connected error is displayed.
9. Verify D16 LED (Encoder Connected) on Chameleon is lit (if using encoder). LED is lit if encoder is sensed by Chameleon.
System reports error if Encoder is selected in user interface and no encoder is sensed. Encoder supply line must be connected
to X25.2 and ground to X25.6 or No Encoder Detected error is displayed.
10. Verify that D6 LED (Trigger Status) goes on and off as you flag the product detector. If the LED does not flash, verify wir -
ing and exchange the product detector.
312
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Web Interface
The laser Web Interface allow users to view or edit laser system settings and is accessible by connecting a PC to the laser via an
Ethernet cable.
Depending on the level that the user logs in as, it is possible to:
l View and edit laser type, head type, software versions, and other details of the laser
l Set the date and time on the laser
l Gain Permissions to fire laser from registered IP address
l Update CMark operating system
l Upload AllPrintBasic files
Connect to the Web Interface
1. Before beginning work, ensure that you know the IP address of the laser system.
2. Set the PC's IP address so that it is on the same subnet as the laser. The first three octets of the IP address are the subnet.
For example: If the laser's IP address is 192.168.1.1 then you set the PC address to 192.168.1.x (x can be any number
from 2 - 254. So your IP address could be set to 192.168.1.5. If the subnet portion of the address (e.g. 192.168.1) is not
the same as the laser you will not establish a connection with the laser using any of the laser software interfaces.
If the laser's IP address is unknown, Dip Switch 1 can be moved to the ON position. After power cycling the laser the IP
address for Ethernet ports will be set to 192.168.1.1.
3. Connect a crossover Ethernet cable to the desired PC/laptop and to the Ethernet port on the rear of the supply unit. Altern -
atively, you can connect the laser side directly to one of the two Ethernet ports on the Chameleon board inside the supply
unit. This may be the case if a network cable were plugged into the standard Ethernet port and the optional user interface
port has a pinned connector.
4. Boot the laser (if not already booted). Do not attempt to connect until the laser has finished boot process and the white
light on the Supply Unit keypad has stopped flashing and is on solid.
5. Launch Internet Explorer program on PC. You may also use Firefox.
6. Type into the address field the IP address of the laser system and press Enter.
Specify Port to Connect to Multi– Core Chameleon Web UI
Multi- Core Chameleon boards host the TCS+ interface on the default HTTP port. You must add port
8080 to the address when entering it in your browser to access the Web UI instead of TCS+, e.g.
192.168.1.1:8080 .
The Web Interface Home screen should appear.
316
Rev AA

Web Interface Tasks
F IGURE 10- 1: Web Interface Home Screen.
Several menu items are available from the Web Interface Home screen. Their functionality depends on whether the user
is logged in or not. Below the top level functions are described. Key functions for service and maintenance personnel are
described in separate sections, these include: getting Permissions, editing the Laser Configuration, uploading an AllPrint
Basic file, and updating CMark software.
Troubleshooting Connection Issues
If unable to connect check the following:
l Are the first three octets of PC's IP address the same as laser and last octet different than laser?
l Was the IP on the correct connection changed?
l Was the IP address for the laser typed in correctly in Internet Explorer address bar?
l Try turning off VPN and wireless.
l Add laser's IP address to list of trusted sites in Internet Explorer | Tools | Security.
l Which position is dip switch #1 in? If in On position connect with default IP address. If in Off position must connect with
customer primary IP address.

lens type, IP address, and if a Pilot Laser is enabled.

Web Interface Tasks
Edit Laser Configuration
You can view or edit system database information through the Laser Web Interface. Should you replace the Chameleon board,
you must replace the default information in the board's system database with the correct settings for the laser in which you have
installed it.
If you do not update system information the data mismatch may cause faults and render the laser inoperable.
Editing the Laser Configuration
The following steps must be accomplished to edit the laser configuration saved in the system database.
1. Boot the laser.
2. Connect to the laser Web Interface and Log In.
3. Edit Laser Configuration
4. Reboot System.
5. Reconnect to the Web Interface and verify the settings were maintained.
Edit Laser Configuration
Login Required
Only specially trained personnel should edit laser configurations. Do not give passwords to untrained per -
sonnel.

Options & Functions
l Laser Type : Selection must match the actual laser type or errors will assert in the user interface.
l Marking Head Type : Selection must match the marking head type in use or errors will assert in the user interface.
l Lens Type : Incorrect lens type may result in distorted markings and incorrect size of available marking field in user inter -
faces (such as SmartGraph).
l Serial Number : Enter the serial number of the laser here. The serial number is found on a sticker located on the supply unit
rear panel.
l Name : Optional, unless using Videojet Remote Service (VRS). When using VRS, this is where the Friendly Name for the laser
is entered.
l Tube Length : Always set to 0 unless otherwise directed by authorized Videojet engineering or technical support
l IP : IP address of the Ethernet port on Chameleon board. There are two Ethernet ports on Chameleon board, but the
IP address will be the same for both ports unless Secondary IP  is enabled. IP addresses are not tied to upper or lower port.
Subnet mask : Enter the subnet mask for Ethernet ports on Chameleon board. Both ports will have the same subnet mask
unless secondary IP address and subnet mask are entered and enabled.
l Secondary IP enabled : When toggled on, you can enter and use a second IP address.
l Secondary IP : If Secondary IP enabled is toggle on, you can enter second IP address here.
l Secondary Subnet mask : Enter the subnet mask for secondary IP address, likely the same as the other IP address unless the
network administrator says otherwise.

Rev AA

Web Interface Tasks
l Gateway : A Gateway is not necessary for direct connections from your laptop, but is necessary when connecting the laser
to a network.
l Timezone : Select the correct timezone from the drop down list.
l Pilot Laser installed : Tells the system whether a pilot laser is installed in the system. Toggle ON if there is a pilot laser
installed or if you have just finished installing one. Options for using pilot laser in user interfaces may not be displayed if
value is toggled OFF.
l Pilot Laser Zero Position X : Used when calibrating pilot laser to match positioning of CO2 laser beam.
l Pilot Laser Zero Position Y : Used when calibrating pilot laser to match positioning of CO2 laser beam.
l Pilot Laser Scaling Factor X : Used to calibrate marking length on X axis to the output mark of CO2 on X axis.
l Pilot Laser Scaling Factor Y : Used to calibrate marking length on Y axis to the output mark of CO2 on X axis.
l Safety Circuit : Select the type of safety circuit used in the system from the dropdown selector. An incorrect setting here
could cause errors or inoperable laser until selection is corrected.
l Leakage Protection Enabled : Always toggled ON. When ON, the shutter will close after 9 seconds of no product move -
ment. The shutter will reopen again when product starts to move. This feature prevents fires due to beam leakage when the
product is stopped below marking head.
l Trigger enable voltage needed : Set to Default (3x40) - true. Lasers other than the 7230/7330 using Chameleon board may
not have Trigger Enable input and may be set to false.
TriggerEnable Voltage Settings
If Trigger Enable Voltage needed is set to Default (3x40) or to True and 24V is not applied to X25.9
then Waiting for Trigger status will appear and no mark will be allowed. This condition applies when
using Product Registration/Line Setup is set to an external source. Ensure that 24V is applied to X25.9
Trigger Enable input in order to avoid constant Waiting for Trigger condition.
l Disable Trigger/Encoder detached errors : Only available since CMark 3.4. Enable if using trigger or encoder signals, but
not using 24V output from X25 connector to power trigger/encoder.
l System Integration Mode : Not functional on Videojet 7230/7330 models.
Disable No Trigger/Encoder Attached
Errors
When an external trigger source or encoder are specified in the Product Registration (Line Setup) the system looks for current
drain on specific outputs of the Customer Interface. The system monitors connector X25 pin 2 for Encoder and pin 4 for Trigger
(Product Detect) for a current draw of at least 20 mA. When the load is not present one of the following error messages is dis -
played: "E10813: No Encoder Connected" or "E10814: No Trigger Detector Connected." An example of this situation would be
using a dry contact for trigger or sharing one encoder for multiple lasers. Starting with CMark version 3.4 these errors may be dis -
abled through the laser's Web Interface.
327
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
CMark Versions Prior to 3.4
Prior to CMark version 3.4 it is necessary to install a 4.7 KOhm resistor on connector X25 pin 2 to 6 if the applic -
ation is not using the Encoder 24V output, but Encoder is selected in Product Registration/Line Setup. If the
application is not using the Trigger 24V output but Product Registration calls for external source, you must
install a 4.7 KOhm resistor from X25 pins 4- 8. In such applications where CMark cannot be updated to version
3.4 then it is still necessary to use resistor (s). The option to Disable Trigger/Encoder Attached Errors will not
appear in Web Interface on lasers with CMark older than version 3.4.
Disabling Trigger/Encoder Detached Errors
1. Connect to Laser Web Interface by opening Internet Explorer and typing in the IP address of the laser.
2. Log in to the Web Interface.
User name service
Password 123@service
3. Select Laser | Config .
4. Click to toggle on Disable Trigger/Encoder Detached Error . A check should appear in the box.
328
Rev AA

Web Interface Tasks
5. Click Save and wait for a prompt to reboot.
6. Reboot the laser. Errors will no longer appear when not using 24V output for the Trigger or Encoder.
Expected Behavior
With errors disabled in the web interface, the following behavior will be exhibited when Encoder or external Trigger are selected in
Product Registration/Line Setup, but not using 24V output. These behaviors will not affect performance of laser:
l Encoder Connected LED (D16) on Chameleon will not illuminate.
l Trigger Connected LED (D20) on Chameleon will not illuminate.
l Encoder ChA Status LED (D18) / Encoder ChaB Status LED (D2) will function normally.
l Trigger Sensor Status LED (D6) on Chameleon will function normally.
l TCS software - Product Detect and/or Encoder green lights in Home Screen and Line Setup will not illuminate.
Updating CMark via the Web Interface
Using the Web Interface, you can update the CMark operating system stored on the laser system SD card without removing the
card. The CMark Update through Web Interface requires that the SD card already has the necessary bootloader file on it either
through having been loaded from Videojet or by using the XMarkCopyTool to load CMark at least once on the SD card.
Loading CMark through the Web Interface is the only option on laser systems with Chameleon boards with multi- core processor
as there is no SD present. CMark is stored in Flash memory and you can only update CMark through the Web Interface.
Restoring Backups
CMark 4.0 and later allows you to backup and restore your CMark. Backup restoration is the same process as
updating except that you choose a backup file instead of an update file.
To update CMark software using the Web Interface you must:
1. Connect to the Web Interface .
2. Update CMark
3. Restart the laser
Required Items
l Flat blade screwdriver for opening supply unit (if backing up AllPrint Basic or tcl files)
l SD card reader (if backing up AllPrint Basic or tcl files)- single core Chameleon only
l Ethernet crossover cable
l CMark software saved locally (SAR version if applicable)
l (When applicable) A previous CMark backup - multi- core Chameleon only

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Select Customer Data Source
In CMark version 4.1 and newer, you may choose where to inherit the customer data from including
from an SD card. The system only recognizes the following sources for inheriting data: internal
(EMMC), SD card, and USB. No other locations may be selected. USB and SD card will only be shown as
options in drop- down menu if the SD or USB is plugged in.
Versions of CMark older than 4.1 do not have this feature.
10. Click the Update button to begin the upgrade process. Click Discard to abort the upgrade process.
The update process may take up to 10 minutes. Be patient.
11. When process is completed screen will show the message "Done" system will go into Standby mode.
12. Press the Power/Standby button to restart the system.
13. Reconnect to the web interface after boot process is complete.
14. Select About and verify that the correct version of CMark is loaded. You do not have to log in to view this information.
CMark Backup
You can create a backup of the CMark files, including the Customer Database, through the Web Interface. For those familiar
with Chameleon or ACC board based laser systems, the result of this process would be the same as making a copy of the SD card.
l The backup will include CMark and all Customer folder information such as Templates/Jobs, Parameter/Mark Sets, Product
Registrations/Line Setups,TCL files, and AllPrintBasic files.
l System Database entries are not included in the CMark Backup. System Database includes the laser type, head type, lens
type, IP addresses, serial number, and laser name.
l Only perform a CMark Backup when the system is in good, working order.
l The backup process can take from 8- 10 minutes.
l A shorter backup of only the customer Database can be done more frequently and independently of CMark Backup to keep
an updated backup of the customer database.
Minimum Version Required
This feature is only available on lasers with CMark version 4.1 and newer and a multi- core Chameleon board
installed. Earlier versions of CMark do not include this option.

1. Boot the laser.
2. Connect to the Web Interface .

Data in the tcl scripts folder will be overwritten

uploaded file.
1. Boot laser.

Only .zip files allowed

plays with "TCL_ " added to the beginning of the name.

Rev AA

l After a successful extraction, the .zip file is automatically deleted.
l Rebooting also clears the temporary memory, so rebooting between uploading and extracting will cause the file to no
longer be available to extract.
l If the upload is unsuccessful, a confirmation dialog containing detailed information of what went wrong will display.
Troubleshooting - File Structure
TCL scripts to be uploaded to the laser must be in the correct format. Following are some of the items necessary for a package
that can be uploaded to the laser system through the Web Interface.
337
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
l A file called startup.sh must exist within the .zip archive. This file is needed in order to tell the laser to run the script. The fol -
lowing figure shows the TCL files and folders used to run Ultimate 6 tcl as an example. There should be no subdirectories
between the .zip being loaded and the startup.sh file within that .zip.
l The .zip package cannot contain parent directories like the example shown below. The script will not run if parent dir -
ectories above the actual TCL package are present.
l A correct .zip package can contain sub- directories within the .zip package if necessary so long as the startup.sh file is at
the root level of the .zip being loaded.

Wireless Client Adapter for Laser Units
A Wireless Access Client (WAC) kit is available specifically for use with lasers using TCS+ or Videojet Remote Service. The WCA kit
is only a wifi adapter that allows a laser to join a LAN via wifi, it does not create a wifi network for you..
The laser wifi kit part number is: AL- 78968.
The kit includes a MikroTik wifi adapter, two Ethernet cables, Power over Ethernet adapter, and multiple AC power cords to power
the adapter. Instructions for setting up the wifi adapter are available with the kit.
343
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
Use AC power only
The wifi adapter should never be powered using the USB port on the Chameleon board. Using the USB for
power can create issues for the laser and/or application as the USB port does not provide enough power for the
MikroTik.
The difference between wifi kit for other Videojet products (Part number: 393916) and the laser wifi kit (AL- 78968) is the inclu -
sion of the multiple AC power cords with the laser specific kit. If 393916 has the AC power cord to fit your region, you can install
that kit to your laser in place of AL- 78968.

Web Interface Tasks
Enable MIC
Either EtherNet/IP or PROFINET industrial protocols are possible when a MIC - EtherNet/IP or MIC- PROFINET board are installed
on the Chameleon MCIP. The MIC must be enabled and configured before use of EtherNet/IP or PROFINET can be accom -
plished.
Two versions of MIC board
There are two different versions of the MIC board: one for EtherNet/IP and one for PROFINET. Each has its
own part number and is specific to the protocol. It is not possible to change the firmware and software on the
MIC if the wrong part is ordered.
After installation of appropriate MIC board, complete the following steps to enable and configure.
1. Connect to Web Interface.
2. Click on Other menu from the home screen.
345

3. Select External Interface from the drop- down menu.

The following steps show EtherNET/IP screens, but there is no difference in procedure for PROFINET.

IP for MIC only if DHCP should not be used.

discovered, system will refresh screen and Status in window will show Active. Additional information such as MAC Address
and Firmware Version will also be populated.

Permissions
Any computer on the same network and subnet as the laser can communicate with the laser system, however you must register a
PC's IP address to the laser to allow fire commands from that PC to the laser. It is not necessary to log into the laser Web Inter -
face to add or edit Permissions to the laser's memory.
Adding an IP address to the laser's Permission may be necessary when:
l Servicing a printer - register your laptop
l Installing a laser system - register the PC or touchscreen that will control laser
Registering Permissions
1. Connect to the laser's Web Interface.
2. Click to select Laser | Permissions from the main menu.
F IGURE 10- 14: Permissions options selected from the Laser drop down menu.
The IP addresses that have Permissions on the laser system will be shown.
From the Current Permissions screen the user may Add, Delete, or Edit the IP addresses that have Permissions on the sys -
tem.

Rev AA

Web Interface Tasks

IP address.
357
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
F IGURE 10- 16: Current Permissions screen with bottom IP address being edited.

your changes.
5. Click Del next to an IP address entry to delete it from the system.

your changes.

Glossary
A
ACC (Advanced Controller Card)
The primary system board of many Videojet lasers. Functionaly equivelant to a motherboard in a PC. The ACC3 is super -
ceded by the Chameleon board.
aperture
An opening, window, or lens through which optical radiation can pass. The aperture limits the energy or power for meas -
urement of exposure. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
B
beam divergence
Divergence is the increase in the diamater of the laser beam with distance from the beam waist, based on the full angle
at the point where the irradiance (or radiant exposure for pulsed lasers) is 1/e times the maximum value. (ANSI 136.1 -
2014)
beam expander
An optical device that expands the size of a collimated beam. There are generally two types, prismatic and telescopic.
Videojet beam expansion technology utlizes the latter telescopic (or Galilean) beam expansion method.
beam waist
The minimum diameter, in centimeters, of a focused, axis- symetric beam. For non- symetric beams, there may be a beam
waist along each major axis, each located at a different distance from the source. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
BTU (Beam Turning Unit)
Beam turning units are precisely machined, modular units containing mirrors inside internal elbow joints. BTUs may be
inserted between the laser head and marking head to redirect marking output to a location otherwise inaccesible to the
full laser head and marking head assembly.
BUS
Beam Unit Straight is a linear tube that allows the marking head to be located away from the laser head thus allowing
the marking head to fit into areas that would be inaccessible to full laser head and marking head assembly. Can be used
369
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
in conjunction with BTUs.
C
Controller
One of four main components of a laser system, the controller is the user interface device by which an operator directly
interacts with the laser system.
CPD
Cobra Power Distribution board is the primary safety controller in Videojet lasers that use Chameleon boards. The CPD
is the main interface for connections for interlock and shutterlock safety systems. The CPD is always present, but if a
higher performance level is necessary then a FASS board may be added to the CPD to bring to Performance Level D.
D
direct marking
From Smartgraph while editing a template, sending a template directly to a connected laser and firing the laser, marking
that template immediately.
DSP
Digital Signal Processor. Controls Laser source control, Marking trigger process, Marking head control, Marking process
E
ellipticity
A measurable ratio of the electromagnetic fields within a beam, relevant in the context of using devices to measure beam
size or power.
expansion factor
The value by which. through the application of a beam expander, the diamater of a laser beam diameter is increased.
F
FASS
FASS - FOBA Alltec Sicherheitssteuerung (Safety Controller)
370
Rev AA

Glossary
focal length
The distance from the secondary nodal point of a lens to the secondary focal point. For a thin lens imagin a distant
source, the focal length is the distance between the lens and the focal point. (ANSI 136.1 - 2014)
G
gain medium
The source of optical gain in the laser, e.g. the contents of the beam source/laser tube.
I
ID Matrix
Another term for a Data Matrix code.
index step
Index steps allow you to use different marking parameters for various objects within the same template.
irradiance
Radiant power incident per unit area upon a surface, expressed in watts- per- centimeter- squared (W*cm^2). (ANSI 136.1
- 2014)
J
jump
A jump is movement of the scanner mirrors from the end of one stroke to the beginning of the next. The laser beam is
OFF during a jump.
jump delay
Jump Delay is a period of time prior to each jump that the galvos are not moved and the laser beam is OFF.
jump speed
Jump Speed is the speed at which the galvos move during a jump.
371
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
L
laser
A device that produces a nearly parallel, nearly monochromatic, and coherent beam of light by exciting atoms to a higher
energy level and causing them to radiate their energy in phase. Originally an acronym standing for (L)ightwave (A)m -
plification by (S)timulated (E)mission of (R)adiation.
laser head
One of four main components in a laser sytem, the laser head contains the laser tube and the Fast Move (FM) board.
laser off delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning off the laser after the galvo mirrors stop moving.
laser on delay
At the start of each stroke, delay turning on the laser after starting to turn the galvo mirrors for marking movement.
Laser Tube
The hermetically sealed tube contaiining primary and secondary gases through which photons pass and are amplified
through stimulated radiation emission.
line width
The Line Width parameter allows you to tell the laser exactly how wide one line marks on the substrate in mm. Greatly
affected to the Overlap parameter.
LVDS
Low Voltage Differential Signal is the means of galvo signal commands from the ACC board to the FM Board or in lasers
with Chameleon boards to the CP- Light board.
M
mark delay
At the the end of each vector, delay the turning the galvo mirrors before marking the next vector.
372
Rev AA

Glossary
marking field
The two dimensional plane across which the laser system can mark. At its center is the origin of the X,Y axis (0,0). The X
and Y values on this axis correspond to the X and Y galvos and the positive/negative signal values they receive to mark
at a given location within the plane.
marking head
One of four main components in a laser system, the marking head contains the X and Y galvos that actively and dynam -
ically direct the beam at the substrate during marking.
marking intensity
Marking intensity is the strength of the beam. In Smartgraph you can adjust marking intensity as a percentage of the max -
imum power of the laser marking system.
marking speed
Marking speed is the speed at which galvos move when the laser beam is on.
Matrix
A matrix splits a single template into multiple discrete cells, each of which can contain unique image and text content or
a repeated pattern of the same image and text content.
MMC
An MMC or Multimedia Card is a flash memory storage device on which the laser operating system is stored. MMCs are
a common flash storage product but have been superseded by SD Cards in both general use and in Videojet lasers.
mode quality
Mode Quality (or M^2) is a measurement of beam quality expressed a ration between the measured beam and a perfect
diffraction- limited Gaussian beam which would have an M^2 = 1.
O
off delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning off the laser after the galvo mirrors stop moving.
on delay
At the start of each stroke, delay turning on the laser after starting to turn the galvo mirrors for marking movement.
373
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
overlap
The Overlap parameter determines how closely to the last line each new line is marked. Overlap is set as a percentage
of total line width from - 50% to 50% with 0% representing no overlap at all.
P
Pilot Laser
A small, visiible laser that demonstrates where the marking head will mark. The pilot laser does not mark on substrate.
polarization
In most cases, laser light is linearly polarized. This means that the electric field oscillates in a particular spatial direction.
pulse frequency
Lasers mark not with a continuous beam but rather with rapid laser pulses (CO2, YAG, Fibers). The pulses are marked
close together and overlap so that each stroke appears to be a solid line. The Pulse Frequency parameter allows your
control over how frequent the pulses are, i.e. how much they will overlap.
R
rise time
The time required for a pulse to rise from 10 percent to 90 percent of its steady value.
S
SD Card
Secure Digital (SD) is a non- volatile memory card format developed by the SD Card Association (SDA) for use in port -
able devices. The standard was introduced in August 1999 by joint efforts between SanDisk, Panasonic (Matsushita
Electric) and Toshiba as an improvement over MultiMediaCards (MMC), [1] and has become the industry standard. SD
Cards supersede MMCs on ACCs revision L or later.
SLEB (Steered Laser Extension Board)
Adds dedicated Ethernet port, Serial connection (RS- 232), and External Job Selection functionality.
spot size
Diameter of the beam as measured at the optimal focal distance lens in use. Spot size is the diameter of a beam at X
inensity at a given range. In this context the X can be defined as [1/e2 x I_ max] which resolves to a diameter across
374
Rev AA

Glossary
which the beam reliably provides 13.5% of the beams maximum intesity. Alteranatively, X may be defined as the half
intensity point, or in other words as Full- width Half- height (FWHH) - the diameter of the beam at which intensity is equal
to 50% of its maximum, center intensity.
stroke
A single, continguous mark made by the laser.
stroke delay
At the end of each stroke, delay turning the galvo mirrors before marking the next stroke.
supply unit
One of four main components in a laser system, the supply unit is a case that houses the processing, memory, and
power supply components.
T
template
A single file that containst the images and text that will be marked by a laser system, created in a design program such
as Smartgraph.
U
Umbilical
The complete assembly of wires and cables that connect a supply unit to the laser head.
V
Vanadate
A compound containing an oxoanion of vanadium generally in its highest oxidation state of +5. Vanadate is found inside
the beam source of YLP lasers.
vector
A vector is one of many small, consecutive line segments that make up a stroke or curve. The number of vectors in a
curve is determined by the design of the font in use and the choices made by the fontographer that created that font.
vector minimum & maximum
375
Rev AA

7330 & 7230 Student Training Guide
W
wavelength
The distance between one peak or crest of a wave and the next peak or crest. It is equal to the speed of the wave divided
by its frequency, and to the speed of a wave times its period.
376
